## Grand Prix Toronto 2012

![](https://i.ormos.uk/4f/b1/4fb139104f583421b5e5acca268d89ef4e236fdfe61ece9bce9bf13ff0436e8b.jpg)

Congratulations to Grand Prix Toronto Champion Willy Edel!

Yes, Edel played Jund, making it the third Modern Grand Prix in a row won by [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and friends, but not looking deeper than that would be a mistake. Not only was Edel's take on Jund truly innovative—[Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) and [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite)!—but the tournament was littered with a variety of archetypes.

This weekend saw the reinvigoration of [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) as an archetype, the rise of one of the first true control decks—Collin Morton's UW [Spreading Seas](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spreading+Seas) list—and plenty of players choosing to try and beat 'em rather than join 'em. Even Edel's Jund deck was an attempt to stay one step ahead of, well, Jund.

But what this weekend may eventually be remembered best for is the continuation of a strong string of performances for Edel, who, since his *Return to Ravnica* Top 8, has been consistently putting up strong finishes, capped by his win this weekend. The pro has been around for a long time, but this season has truly been one to remember.

So congratulations to Willy Edel and all of the other Top 8 competitors at Grand Prix Toronto!

## Quarterfinals - Dan Jordan vs. Alon Chitiz

*by Blake Rasmussen*

Alon Chitiz is something of an amazing story for this tournament. He had zero byes and is mostly a casual player. But, playing [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) has had its rewards this weekend, as two variations of the deck made the Top 8, and a number of others were close behind. His careful play and knowledge of the deck, however, has set Chitiz apart.

Dan Jordan, meanwhile, has made a name for himself on the Star City Games circuit and had a string of solid finishes at the Professional and Grand Prix levels, including a Top 4 at Indianapolis in 2012.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/24/46/2446151f91788a09ba2c7fc9c58f7319813eb5f562c1ee0fdeaa4ba9d91d6adc.jpg)  
*Dan Jordan*

### Game 1

Jordan was forced to mulligan, but opened a pretty typical Jund hand with three lands, [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt), [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) and the [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) he played turn one.

[Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand) stalled a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) for a turn and dug Chitiz a little deeper, and a second [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand) kept [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) from the battlefield for a turn. Chitiz found a third [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand) of [Peer Through Depths](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Peer+Through+Depths) and once again sent [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) off to Jordan's hand. But Jordan was able to finally successfully land his [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) to put some pressure on the board.

[Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm) swept away Jordan's pair of [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman)s, leaving [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) Alone on the board. Two turns later Chitiz found [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) with seven lands for the win.

Chitiz 1 – Jordan 0

![](https://i.ormos.uk/41/1b/411bcb96d9767ab1cc6ce4142ef8b4d9abcdc6386a420d77cd5a43e55d32abae.jpg)  
*Alon Chitiz*

### Game 2

Jordan was in hot water right off the bat in game two, forced to mulligan a poor hand into a one-lander, missing on his first try. By the time Stern found his second land, Chitiz was ready with a [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand) for the waiting [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant).

But Jordan started fighting back with a [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) and more lands. But, at this point, it was mostly just a matter of Chitiz finding a [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) over the next few turns. [Peer Through Depths](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Peer+Through+Depths) didn't immediately turn one up, nor did [Serum Visions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serum+Visions), and Chitiz was left to just shake his head in frustration. All he could muster was a [Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm) to kill [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman).

Jordan, meanwhile, started beating in with a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) and a freshly cast [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), but [Snapcaster Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapcaster+Mage) on [Peer Through Depths](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Peer+Through+Depths) finally found the [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift). From there, it was all math.

Chitiz 2 – Jordan 0

## Quarterfinals - Jon Stern vs. Sam Pardee

*by Blake Rasmussen*

Sam Pardee vs. Jon Stern was one of the maquee matchups of the weekend, as Melira Pod, a deck generally believed to be favored against Jund, squared off against the format-defining menace.

### Game 1

Pardee owned the early game against Stern, playing Deatrite Shaman into an [Orzhov Pontiff](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orzhov+Pontiff), killing Stern's second turn [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), followed by a [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) that sacrificed the Pontiff to find a [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap).

Meanwhile, Stern was in a rough spot. He had managed to resolve [Liliana of the Veil](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+of+the+Veil), but was short on ways to protect it. He did [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) off a [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), seeing another [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) and [Reveillark](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reveillark). Combined with Liliana, both hit the graveyard.

Pardee then used [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) to search up Meliria, [Sylvok Replica](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvok+Replica), one card away from comboing Pardee out relatively quickly. An attack with the now night immortal Redcap killed Liliana.

But Stern wasn't out. [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) was sitting in his hand, ready to take out the 2/2 Legend.

But it wasn't needed, at least not yet. Pardee chose to use [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) to sacrifice Melira herself to find a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks).

Stern responded in kind with a [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) off the top of his deck, chumping with a spirit the following turn.

But [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) kept working its magic. It turned [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) into [Ranger of Eos](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ranger+of+Eos), which dug up a [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) and [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer). Pardee was working toward his combo, but he also had an active [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township) to press Stern from both directions.

One turn later, Pardee was able to assemble his combo without much resistance from Stern.

Pardee 1 – Stern 0

![](https://i.ormos.uk/14/17/1417c25dfb92a34a8482f4a3ca96c8ad6effb6985a6ff5d35d0651967ff0f791.jpg)  
*Jon Stern*

### Game 2

Once again Pardee started quickly, casting two [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) and a [Darkblast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darkblast) on Stern's [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant). Stern reloaded with a [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls), but his big play was [Olivia Voldaren](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Olivia+Voldaren), which could shut down Pardee's entire deck on its own.

Sure enough, Olivia took out two [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) before Pardee was able to use [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) and [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) to find a [Phyrexian Metamorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Phyrexian+Metamorph) and trade with the Legendary Vampire.

[Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) then started doing work, finding [Ranger of Eos](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ranger+of+Eos), which, in turn, dug up a pair of [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman)s.

But by then the damage had built up too much from [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) and [Olivia Voldaren](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Olivia+Voldaren) attacks. When Pardee couldn't stop the damage or combo off in time, Stern evened things up at a game each.

Pardee 1 – Stern 1

![](https://i.ormos.uk/43/b3/43b3bac21ff6fa2b7bc42abbf3105c499a9ef6aa7d66951c666300e227f7aef1.jpg)  
*Sam Pardee*

### Game 3

Pardee led with a [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) which, as appropriate, was promptly hit with [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt). With nothing else to do, Pardee simply replaced it with a second copy on his turn, following up with a [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks).

Stern, meanwhile, played [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) into [Stony Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stony+Silence), pressuring Pardee AND turning off Pardee's best card.

Unpreturbed, Pardee simply started making [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks). Finks has repeatedly been cited as one of the best cards against Jund, and the pair of them were putting Stern's resolve to the test. It looked like it would be a complete grind between two decks trading one-for-ones

Then Pardee cast [Melira, Sylvok Outcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Melira%2C+Sylvok+Outcast) and [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer) and gained "nine million, billion life." So there's that.

And even though Stern cast [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) on [Melira, Sylvok Outcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Melira%2C+Sylvok+Outcast)—successfully—it didn't much seem to matter at this point. Eventually Pardee found a Redcap to take the match.

Pardee 2 – Stern 1

## Quarterfinals - Willy Edel (Jund) vs. James Vance (Scapeshift)

*by Mike Rosenberg*

Willy Edel has been on a roll this season. Following a Top 8 finish at Pro Tour *Return to Ravnica*, he's tweaked his Jund deck to take the latest evolutions of the format head-on. Packing [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) and [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite), his deck has been accelerating into big plays and sweeping away spirit tokens and [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) all weekend. His opponent, James Vance, has also had plenty of success this weekend with [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift), one of the big decks to see a resurgence at the top tables here at GP Toronto.

Will Edel's disruption and power-packed plays be enough to get him and his Jund deck into the Semi Finals, or would Vance's [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) be too quick?

### Game 1

Vance had the advantage of winning the die roll, but lacked the sufficient ramp spells to get his deck moving. Edel, meanwhile, had some early action with [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra), [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks), and [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf) into [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman), giving him an early lead.

To make matters worse for Vance, he had no land drop for his fifth turn, instead playing [Sakura-Tribe Elder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sakura-Tribe+Elder) and using Into The Roil to temporarily disperse [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/88/72/8872465997432f1043c76c25488be2369e2ff2f5881dc4d8020c8d79cfb17f90.jpg)  
*Willy Edel once again demonstrates his mastery with the Jund deck in Modern.*

Edel, who was content with going aggressive, sent his creatures in after using a [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) to take out Auger of Bolas. Vance was able to get up to six lands, but Edel's board continued to grow thanks to some assistance from [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra). Vance attempted to buy time with a desperation [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command), but a [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) activation followed before the attack step, leaving Vance down and out in the first game.

Edel 1 – Vance 0

### Game 2

Vance led off with a slow start, but Edel's start wasn't any faster, with the first moment of action taking place during the third turn. An Auger of Bolas on the fourth turn found nothing for Vance, who lost the Merfolk to a [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) at the end of his turn.

Edel's first big turn took place with everyone's favorite *Alara Reborn* cascade creature, [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf). It found a [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt), which was pointed at Vance, but Vance had a Bolt of his own to take out the hasty elf. Vance continued to play lands, content with Edel not having anything.

Edel tried for another [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), but found nothing he could profitably cast, as some removal went to the bottom. The elf then met its end on the stack, succumbing to Vance's [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command). Vance followed with [Cultivate](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cultivate), finding some lands, which went along with his [Sakura-Tribe Elder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sakura-Tribe+Elder).

Edel found and cast [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) on the next turn, following up with [Marsh Flats](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Marsh+Flats) for some free mana. It was used to wake up a [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) in. [Sakura-Tribe Elder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sakura-Tribe+Elder) jumped in the way, finding a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest). Vance cleared the [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) away with [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout), and then took a hit from Edel's [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) on the next turn, with Vance's [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift)s hiding from him in his deck.

Vance dug with a [Peer Through Depths](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Peer+Through+Depths), but again [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) did not arrive. Instead he found [Unsummon](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unsummon), which returned Edel's [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) when it woke up on the next turn.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3c/e0/3ce0b69532e4bea339d1045f381cfba8e6460746dc08050282d860c101a6d1df.jpg)  
*Vance buys time in every way that he can, knowing that one good draw step will win him the game.*

Vance drew again, this time finding Auger of Bolas. The Auger found [Peer Through Depths](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Peer+Through+Depths), which then found [Rude Awakening](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rude+Awakening). Vance, however, was short on mana to cast it, so he passed...

...and Edel happily obliged with [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), as Vance sank back in his seat, his game-ending sorcery discarded. The [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) followed through for another attack, putting Vance to 7. Vance had [Sakura-Tribe Elder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sakura-Tribe+Elder) on the next turn, but Edel [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt)ed the Auger of Bolas, and his [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) and [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) crashed in. Vance was forced to block, shuffling up his deck. He had to draw a [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) on the next turn.

However, he found something just as good. [Snap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snap)ecaster Mage let him flash back an entwined [Rude Awakening](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rude+Awakening), which let Vance's lands rumble over to win the game.

Edel 1 – Vance 1

### Game 3

Edel was first to act with [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), drawing a [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) from Vance on the second turn. Edel fired back with [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), passing back with a fetch land lying in wait. Meanwhile, Vance was content to cast [Sakura-Tribe Elder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sakura-Tribe+Elder), which jumped in front of [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) on the next turn. Edel continued to deploy threats with a second [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) before passing action back to Vance. It was no matter to Vance, content with casting [Sakura-Tribe Elder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sakura-Tribe+Elder), passed with a potentially lethal [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) in hand.

Well, that was the case. At least until Edel played [Slaughter Games](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slaughter+Games) naming [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift). Vance slumped into his seat. It was now going to be [Rude Awakening](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rude+Awakening) or bust for the [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) player.

![Slaughter Games](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Slaughter+Games)

[Slaughter Games](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Slaughter+Games): pretty good against [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift).

The [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf)s rumbled in, as Vance's second [Sakura-Tribe Elder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sakura-Tribe+Elder) jumped in the way.

Vance found an [Augur of Bolas](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Augur+of+Bolas), and attempted to dig for his remaining out. He found nothing, and was forced to pass. The [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf)s attacked again, the Merfolk jumping in the way, as Vance fell to 11. [Firespout](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Firespout) and a [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) were used to dispatch a [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), but Edel continued his assault, dropping Vance to 7 before playing [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra). His creatures were tapped down by [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command), and a post-combat [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) forced another [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) from Vance.

Vance bought a little more time with [Sakura-Tribe Elder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sakura-Tribe+Elder) blocking [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), but when the top of his deck revealed no way out, Vance offered the handshake on the next turn.

Edel 2 – Vance 1

## ) vs. Sam Pardee (Melira-Pod)

*by Mike Rosenberg*

Alon Chitiz and his [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) deck and Sam Pardee with Melira-Pod will clash combo against combo in the Semi Finals. Would Chitiz be able to reach the necessary numbers of lands before Pardee assembles a monstrous combination with Melira and her best friends, [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer) and [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) and/or [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap)? Let's find out!

### Game 1

Pardee was the first to act in the match with a first-turn [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise), as he was sitting with two-thirds of his combo in hand and ready to go. Chitiz had his first play in the form of [Sakura-Tribe Elder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sakura-Tribe+Elder), while Pardee played out [Melira, Sylvok Outcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Melira%2C+Sylvok+Outcast).

Chitiz sacrificed his [Sakura-Tribe Elder](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sakura-Tribe+Elder) for a land, and then followed up with a third-turn [Pyroclasm](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyroclasm). Pardee rebuilt with [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise), but his replacement Melira was [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand)ed. Chitiz suspended [Search for Tomorrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Search+for+Tomorrow), while Pardee played out the rest of his hand with Melira, [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer), and a [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township), one of the breakout successes for the Pod decks this weekend.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/53/d1/53d1011626c998f30a7ab0612041b3ac1828c1114f066fde2684d099fbd793e8.jpg)  
*Pardee, content with [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township), but happiest with a combo.*

While Chitiz was content with building his mana, Pardee got to work with his [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township), using it before sending Melira and the Seer in for 5 points of damage. Chitiz found his land, and then found... more ramp, as [Farseek](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Farseek) fetched out yet another land to add to Chitiz's table. He passed, still looking for a [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift).

When Pardee went to combat, a [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command) from Chitiz tapped down Pardee's board... and with the coast now clear, Pardee cast [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap), and demonstrated the combo with Melira and the Seer. Chitiz nodded, and picked up his cards.

Chitiz 0 – Pardee 1

### Game 2

Chitiz began with his ideal start of [Search for Tomorrow](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Search+for+Tomorrow) suspended and Sakura Tribe Elder, but Pardee also had a fast opening with [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) into [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod). On the third turn, Chitiz had five lands in play. [Serum Visions](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Serum+Visions) drew him a card, and he left one card on top with his scry 2 before playing a land and passing.

[Ethersworn Canonist](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ethersworn+Canonist) from Pardee met [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand), and a second [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) along with [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer) came down after. [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) upgraded one of the Birds into [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots), which then made a mana so Pardee could cast another Bird.

Chitiz cast [Telling Time](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Telling+Time), then paid 2 to play [Stomping Ground](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stomping+Ground)s untapped. He passed, and when Pardee attempted a second [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod), Chitiz was ready with [Remand](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Remand).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c9/c8/c9c89e2b3bc65514e3293a2069794298baa6d9ffb3ed055123e2581b4c5039d2.jpg)  
*Chitiz digs for more ways to buy time with his [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) deck."*

However, with the coast now clear, Pardee cast [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), depriving Chitiz of a dangerous [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift). [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer) swung in for 1, and [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) became [Melira, Sylvok Outcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Melira%2C+Sylvok+Outcast) courtesy of [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) on the next turn.

When Chitiz passed with no other action, Pardee used [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) to trade in [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) for [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks). His combo now assembled, Pardee felt no pressure as he attacked with his creatures, and sat with his combo in play, waiting for Chitiz to do something before gaining an arbitrarily large amount of life.

With no way to react and behind on the board, Chitiz was forced to act when Pardee turned an [Ethersworn Canonist](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ethersworn+Canonist) on the next turn into another [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks). When Chitiz attempted for [Cryptic Command](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cryptic+Command), Pardee responded with sacrificing his already in-play [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks), looping for millions of life, and earning the concession from Chitiz.

Chitiz 0 – Pardee 2

## Semifinals - Willy Edel vs. Alex Majlaton

*by Blake Rasmussen*

Willy Edel has been on an absolute tear lately, especially in Modern and especially with Jund. He's been playing the deck in some variation of it since Pro Tour *Return to Ravnica* (where he made the Top 8), and has [a unique perspective](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://magic.wizards.com/en/articles/archive/event-coverage/grand-prix-toronto-day-2-blog-2012-12-09) on the archetype and how to build and play the deck. If anyone knows Jund, it's Willy Edel.

And if you need someone to talk to about Robots, you'd be hard pressed to do better than Alex Majlaton. The four-time Grand Prix Top 8 participant says he's "been obsessing over it for 8 years" and has shown deft command of the deck this weekend on his way to the semifinals.

One of them, however, had to budge.

### Game 1

Majlaton led with [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest) off [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus) and a [Mox Opal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mox+Opal), then saw his hand revealed with a [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize) on Edel's first turn. It revealed:

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3d/51/3d512ea75193fb5c2140f514871682b88fe8d45762d35b246d2713ef277c285d.jpg)  
*[Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium), [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating), [Springleaf Drum](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Springleaf+Drum) and [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island)*

The hand gave Edel plenty of pause. Both Plating and Master were deadly, but Edel eventually opted to send the [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium) to the graveyard.

[Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) predictably entered play and suited up on the Pest, hitting for three damage.

Edel, content to sit on [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt), simply played [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) and passed.

Playing [Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer) and the [Springleaf Drum](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Springleaf+Drum), Majlaton attempted to strike for five the next turn, but Edel used his Bolt to take out the [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest), followed by another [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) the next turn to kill the [Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer).

Edel had certainly weathered the initial storm, but Majlaton wasn't done yet. A [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium) off the top of his deck yielded a rather large attacker, and even enabled an attack with [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus) for two.

Fortunately for Edel, his Bloodbraid Elves are better than that, turning up [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) as if on command to oust the [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating).

Still, the [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium) and [Blinkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blinkmoth+Nexus) hit for seven the following turn, dropping Edel to just seven life. He could stall a bit with his [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman)s, but he had to answer the [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium) soon. Chump blocking would work in the short run, but it wouldn't get him very far.

[Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) enabled an attack with a [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) and [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf), with the Elf trading with a lowly [Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite). Unfortunately for Edel, he was completely out of cards at this point and still facing a very large [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium).

[Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) jumped in front of the Master the following turn to stay alive at six life, but he needed something, anything, that could handle [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium).

[Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) was the answer. In response to a second [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium), Edel pulled the trigger on killing the first one. He had bought a reprieve, but merely traded one giant artifact creature for, well, the same giant artifact creature.

All this time, Edel was chipping away at Majlaton's life total with [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) as he could, getting him to just 9 life.

This time, the top of Edel's deck was not so generous, turning up a lame-duck [Thoughtseize](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtseize), only good for some self-inflicted pain were he to cast it (he didn't).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/1e/1f/1e1f1a7bba93cca7244c8001fbd85723499eeaa7d6a4adfb9eada5e47e31649f.jpg)  
*If it looks like Alex Majlaton is winning this game, look again.*

Instead, he sacrificed his [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) to yet another chump block, then put Majlaton to five life with two [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) activations.

Then he drew [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) and won the game.

You read that right. Edel actually won that game.

One Bolt of lightning and two [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) activations later and Majlaton found himself at exactly zero life.

Edel 1 – Majlaton 0

### Game 2

Majlaton had to think about it, but ultimately kept his opening seven. Edel, on the other hand, didn't hesitate to go to six, though he obviously wasn't happy with those six cards, as they had no black mana and only two [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine)s for land.

Majlaton, meanwhile, started with a [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge) into a [Cranial Plating](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cranial+Plating) and [Etched Oracle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Etched+Oracle). More or less the nuts.

Edel tried to fight back with [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls), but when Majlaton played [Master of Etherium](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Master+of+Etherium), Edel had seen enough.

Edel 1 – Majlaton 1

### Game 3

Both players kept for the decider, and Majlaton came roaring out again, dumping [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest), [Mox Opal](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mox+Opal) and [Springleaf Drum](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Springleaf+Drum) onto the table in quick succession, followed by an [Etched Oracle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Etched+Oracle), pretty much the bane of Jund decks everywhere.

And while Edel wasn't quite as fast out of the blocks, his turn two [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) promised potential fireworks on turn three. He used his cornucopia of mana to cast and flash back [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls), littering the board with tokens that could hold back everything but the [Etched Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Etched+Champion). Yet, an attack by the Champion also opened Majlaton up to a large counterattack.

But the Robots mage swung in anyway before playing [Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer) and passing the turn. Edel, despite facing an [Etched Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Etched+Champion), actually looked to hold a strong position, on that only got stronger when he cast both [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse) and [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) on his turn, then attacked with his team. [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) even destroyed the Nexus waiting to ambush his Cobra.

Majlaton, down but not out, reloaded with [Thoughtcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thoughtcast) into a second [Etched Champion](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Etched+Champion) and an [Ornithopter](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ornithopter).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/88/72/8872465997432f1043c76c25488be2369e2ff2f5881dc4d8020c8d79cfb17f90.jpg)  
*When Willy Edel is piloting Jund, it's not a good bet to go against him, no matter how bad things may look.*

At this point, life totals stood at 14-12 in Majlaton's favor.

Edel attacked fearlessly with his [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) brigade, then used [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) to kill [Signal Pest](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Signal+Pest) and blunt Majlaton's attack somewhat. Still, Edel fell to 8 on the attack, and Majlaton cast a [Steel Overseer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Steel+Overseer). Edel was empty handed and seemly falling behind. He would need more help from his library to stay in this one.

And lo! Off the top of Edel's deck came a [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite), giving him a route to victory by attacking with everything.

![Thundermaw Hellkite](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Thundermaw+Hellkite)

Here I come to save the day! It seems that [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite) is on his way!

[Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) cleared out a Nexus capable of blocking, and Majlaton fell to two on the attack.

When he found nothing on top of his library, Majlaton extended his hand. Willy Edel was moving on to the finals of Grand Prix Toronto!

Mike Rosenberg

Edel 2 – Majlaton 1

## Finals - Willy Edel (Jund) vs. Sam Pardee (Melira-Pod)

*by Mike Rosenberg*

Willy Edel, who has been piloting Jund in all of his major Modern events for the season, has proven that he is one of the driving forces behind innovations for the deck. Armed with a powerful list that packs [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) and [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite), new additions to the archetype, he has gone through one opponent after enough all weekend.

But his opponent, Sam Pardee, has proven that he is no slouch with the Modern metagame. Packed with a dangerous version of [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) using [Melira, Sylvok Outcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Melira%2C+Sylvok+Outcast) with [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer) and various persist creatures, the deck is capable of some speedy and resilient wins.

Will the Pro Tour regular from Brazil come out on top, or will the player who was sitting in the top seed for most of the event finish ahead?

### Game 1

Pardee led off with [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise), which Edel quickly destroyed with [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt). Pardee replaced his mana-bird with [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman). Edel, when he played [Inquisition of Kozilek](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inquisition+of+Kozilek), shrugged when Pardee revealed two lands and two [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod)s, causing his discard spell to whiff.

Pardee played [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) on turn 3 after losing his [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) to another [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt), while Edel had a bigger turn 3 with [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra), with a fetch land and activation to make enough mana for [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9b/b7/9bb7d382d04c4486a12a8e27a01614dfc5e8baf30a2b2ddfb3910449bfe30e03.jpg)  
*Sam Pardee gets off to a fast start, using [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) to turn smaller creatures into bigger and better ones.*

Pardee followed up with [Orzhov Pontiff](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orzhov+Pontiff), dispatching Edel's [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra), before sacrificing it to the [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) for [Ranger of Eos](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ranger+of+Eos), which fetched up [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) and [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer). Edel continued to press on with his haunted [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) (courtesy of [Orzhov Pontiff](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orzhov+Pontiff)), and a follow-up [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls).

Pardee, however, played [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) and [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) before passing back to Edel, who had nothing more to go with his flashed back [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls). Pardee traded away a newly played [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) on the next turn for [Melira, Sylvok Outcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Melira%2C+Sylvok+Outcast), assembling his combo, as he passed, waiting for Edel to make the first move.

When Edel chose to do nothing, Pardee used [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) to trade [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) for [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite), then used [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) for four to get [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap). Now, with multiple persist creatures in play, Pardee went for it. He sacrificed [Kitchen Finks](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kitchen+Finks) first, declaring the persist trigger as Edel... laughed and picked up his cards for the next game, sitting with nothing instant speed in hand.

Edel 0 – Pardee 1

### Game 2

Edel led with a [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), which promptly dropped to Pardee's second-turn [Abrupt Decay](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Abrupt+Decay) (off of [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise)). Edel had no other follow-up, and a third-turn [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) traded in [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) for [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots), as Pardee fell to 14. Edel showed why he had no plays when he used [Rakdos Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Charm) to smash the Pod at the end of the turn. However, Edel's turn four [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) met an early demise when Pardee played out [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) for the full cost.

Edel, who was under a lot less pressure with the [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) on his opponent's field, sent in a [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine), dropping Pardee to 10. Pardee, who went aggressive with [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township), sent in a fearsome 4/3 [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw), throwing the [Wall of Roots](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Wall+of+Roots) in front of the [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/56/90/569026162b4678bcb23a8e976886843567a361d5b1771c585fb1b22e2dc5c09a.jpg)  
*Willy Edel plays cautiously, focusing on each play with precision.*

Edel followed with [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer) after his attack before passing, happy with his ever-growing [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine). Pardee played another land, and then gave up his attack in order to convoke a [Chord of Calling](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chord+of+Calling) for 4. This gave Pardee [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap), which quickly dispensed of Edel's [Grim Lavamancer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grim+Lavamancer).

Now, with [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township) available, [Murderous Redcap](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Murderous+Redcap) could prove to be a great chump blocker for Edel's [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine), as the Brazilian pro thought long about his decision. He ultimately woke up [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village). Pardee blocked with the Redcap and persisted to finish off Edel's Treeop Village, but Edel quickly got rid of the Redcap with [Maelstrom Pulse](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Maelstrom+Pulse), ensuring no combos or [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township) plays could happen.

Pardee pressed on with [Orzhov Pontiff](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Orzhov+Pontiff), attacking with a 5/4 [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw). Edel shrugged and sent in the [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine), as Pardee blocked with his Pontiff. Edel passed back, as Pardee found a [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod). He played it and passed back with [Shriekmaw](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shriekmaw) untapped.

Edel thought for a moment before using [Rakdos Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Charm) to crush the new [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod). When he drew, he found a [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt), which cleared the way for his [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) to wrap things up.

Edel 1 – Pardee 1

### Game 3

Pardee led off the third and final game of Grand Prix Toronto with [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer). It attacked for 1, but Pardee's [Melira, Sylvok Outcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Melira%2C+Sylvok+Outcast) elicited a [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) from Edel, as Pardee sacrificed the legendary creature to scry 1. Edel's first creature on board was [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), as Pardee passed without a land on the third turn.

  
[Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant) did what he did best, as he started drawing Edel some cards. Pardee drew, and while he didn't find a land, he did find a [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) for [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant), taking 2 from his Phyrexian mana. The [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer) attacked, bringing Edel to 16, who quickly untapped to play [Bloodbraid Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodbraid+Elf). The elf cascaded into [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra), and her attack dropped Pardee even lower, who still didn't have a third land.

Edel [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember)ed the [Viscera Seer](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Viscera+Seer), and an attack send Pardee to 5. Another turn and a [Treetop Village](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Treetop+Village) activation later, and Pardee offered the handshake.

![](http://magic.wizards.com/sites/mtg/files/image_legacy_migration/mtg/images/daily/events/gptor12/willy_edel_champion620.JPG)  
*Congratulations to Willy Edel, Grand Prix Toronto 2012 champion!*

## The Top 5 Cards of Grand Prix Toronto 2012

*by Blake Rasmussen*

![Scapeshift](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Scapeshift)

5\. [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift)

Made possible by the unbanning of [Valakut, the Molten Pinnacle](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Valakut%2C+the+Molten+Pinnacle), [Scapeshift](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scapeshift) really has not had a chance to shine since then. Either players were packing enough hate to keep the deck down or it just didn't have the polish necessary to make it a force in the metagame. Either way, the deck showed its power this weekend, placing two players in the Top 8. The deck gets a ton of love from the people who play it, and everyone else is certainly going to have to respect its power going forward.

![Birthing Pod](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Birthing+Pod)

4\. [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod)

Speaking of respecting power, Ari Lax—on his way to a Top 16 finish—declared Kiki Pod decks to be the best deck in Modern when [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) was in play. Ultimately, it was the flavor that utilized [Melira, Sylvok Outcast](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Melira%2C+Sylvok+Outcast) that made the finals, but there was no denying that either deck was often seen running laps around helpless opponents who couldn't remove the Phyrexian artifact. Giving Kiki Pod decks the ability to combo seemingly out of nowhere and all flavors of the deck the ability to get incremental value out of every one of their creatures in an overwhelming fashion, [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) is surely on the short list of most powerful things you can do in Modern.

![Gavony Township](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Gavony+Township)

3\. [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township)

If there was one innovation that put [Birthing Pod](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birthing+Pod) decks over the top, it was [Gavony Township](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gavony+Township). But it doesn't end there. GW aggressive decks picked up on the power of the card, as did various other strategies. It was the plan B that is occasionally better than many decks' plan A, turning the format's menagerie of small mana critters into something you can't ignore. And when the most played deck in the format is built on killing things with 1-for-1 removal spells, making every [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) matter is incredibly important.

![Lingering Souls](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Lingering+Souls)

2\. [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls)

It's actually getting a little annoying to write about [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) at this point. The card is so good across so many formats, that we write [entire articles](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://magic.wizards.com/en/articles/archive/event-coverage/grand-prix-toronto-day-1-blog-2012-12-09) on it before the first day is even halfway done. Powering up Jund, Junk, Melissa DeTora's BW Tokens, Collin Morton's UW control deck (splashing black specifically for [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls)), and a variety of other decks, [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) was everywhere this weekend. It's presence could also be felt in the relative lack of Poison decks, which traditionally have a hard time with the card, and the very saucy [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite)s in champion Willy Edel's deck.

![Lotus Cobra](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Lotus+Cobra)

1\. [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra)

If you didn't read [Willy Edel's deck tech](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://magic.wizards.com/en/articles/archive/event-coverage/grand-prix-toronto-day-2-blog-2012-12-09#1208) then you might have missed that Edel actually played [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) over multi-format all-star and possible best two drop of all time [Dark Confidant](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dark+Confidant). Why? Speed, speed, speed. Edel wanted more ways to match the speed of a format practically defined by one-mana accelerators, adding the mythic Cobra to his already full suite of [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman)s. Not only did they kick start Edel's deck, they enabled him to play more expensive cards like [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite). In fact, it was the acceleration of [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) that enabled Edel to keep pace with the otherwise faster Robots and Melira Pod opponents he played against in the last two rounds. It's pretty safe to say that if Edel hadn't been playing [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) this weekend, we very well could be congratulating a completely different champion. Instead, [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20170803151836/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) and Willy Edel will go down as two of the biggest stories from Grand Prix Toronto.
